import { getConfigBasePath, getApiConfigPath } from './config.js'; // .js is correct for NodeNext module resolution

async function ensureDirs() {
  try {
    const baseConfigPath = getConfigBasePath(); // Should create c:/xampp/htdocs/dcr/config
    console.log(`Base config path ensured: ${baseConfigPath}`);
    const apiPath = getApiConfigPath(); // Should create c:/xampp/htdocs/dcr/config/apis
    console.log(`API config path ensured: ${apiPath}`);
  } catch (error) {
    console.error('Failed to ensure config paths:', error);
    process.exit(1);
  }
}

ensureDirs();
```

## 7: SHARED - FX, Tasks, Docs, Collections

### `core\fx.d.ts`

```ts
// Path: core/fx.d.ts

// This tells TypeScript that there's a module at this path.
declare module '@/core/fx.js' {
    // Define the structure of an fx node.
    // We use `any` in many places because fx is highly dynamic.
    // A more advanced implementation could use generics.
    export interface FxNode {
        // --- Private Properties ---
        __id: string;
        __parent_id: string | null;
        __value: Record<string, any>;
        __nodes: Record<string, FxNode>;
        __type: string | null;
        __proto: string | FxNode | null;
        __watchers: Set<(newValue: any, oldValue: any, node: FxNode) => void>;
        __processes: Map<string, (...args: any[]) => Promise<any>>;
        __state: string;

        // --- Core Methods ---
        set(value: any): this;
        val<T>(defaultValue?: T): T;
        type(newType?: string): string | this;
        inherit(protoNode: FxNode | string): this;

        // --- Reactivity ---
        watch(callback: (newValue: any, oldValue: any, node: FxNode) => void): () => void;
        notify(oldValue: any, newValue: any): void;

        // --- Process & Selection ---
        flow(name: string, processFn: (...args: any[]) => Promise<any>): this;
        execute(name: string, ...args: any[]): Promise<any>;
        select(selector: string): FxNode[];

        // --- Serialization / Hydration ---
        wrap(): Record<string, any>;
        expand(data: Record<string, any>): this;

        // --- Dynamic Properties ---
        [key: string]: any;
    }

    // Define the shape of the main `fx` object/proxy.
    export interface Fx {
        // Accessor function
        (path: string, value?: any): FxNode;

        // Static methods
        createNode(parentId?: string | null): FxNode;
        register(classObj: any, name?: string): void;
        select(selector: string): FxNode[];
        query(selector: string): FxNode[];
        classRegistry: Map<string, any>;

        // Top-level node access
        [key: string]: FxNode | any;
    }

    // Define the shape of the DOM selector `$`
    export interface FxDomSelector {
        (selector: string): FxNode | FxNode[] | null;
    }

    // Define the shape of the graph selector `$$$`
    export interface FxGraphSelector {
        (selector: string): FxNode[];
    }

    // Export the globals
    export const fx: Fx;
    export const $$: Fx;
    export const $: FxDomSelector;
    export const $$$: FxGraphSelector;

    // Export the factory function as well
    export function createNode(parentId?: string | null): FxNode;
    export const classRegistry: Map<string, any>;

    export default fx;
}
```


### `core\fx.js`

```js
/**
 * @file fx.js
 * @description Complete FX Framework - Dynamic, Composable, Isomorphic Runtime
 * 
 * Features:
 * - Dynamic Node Creation with Proxy-based access
 * - Effects System for reactive behaviors
 * - Isomorphic Module Loading (Client/Server)
 * - Serialization/Deserialization (wrap/expand)
 * - ExecutionContext for async management
 * - Resource Factory pattern
 * - Manifest-based configuration
 * - DOM Integration
 * - CSS-like querying
 */

// Node.js-only imports removed for browser compatibility.
// Use browser crypto for UUIDs if available, otherwise fallback.
const randomUUID = (typeof crypto !== 'undefined' && crypto.randomUUID)
  ? () => crypto.randomUUID()
  : () => Math.random().toString(36).slice(2) + Date.now();

// createRequire is only available on the server. Guard with IS_SERVER below.

// Environment Detection
const IS_SERVER = typeof window === 'undefined';
const IS_CLIENT = !IS_SERVER;

// Server-side require helper
let requireSync;
if (typeof window === 'undefined') {
    try {
        // Only import/createRequire if in Node.js
        // eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-var-requires
        const { createRequire } = require('module');
        requireSync = createRequire(import.meta.url);
    } catch (e) {
        console.warn('FX-WARN: Server-side module loading may be limited.');
        requireSync = () => { throw new Error('requireSync not available.'); };
    }
}

// Client-side module loader with SharedArrayBuffer
class ClientModuleLoader {
    constructor() {
        this.worker = null;
        this.sharedBuffer = null;
        this.lock = null;
        this.initWorker();
    }

    initWorker() {
        if (!IS_CLIENT) return;
        
        try {
            // Check for SharedArrayBuffer support
            if (typeof SharedArrayBuffer === 'undefined') {
                console.warn('FX-WARN: SharedArrayBuffer not available. Module loading will be async.');
                return;
            }

            this.sharedBuffer = new SharedArrayBuffer(1024 * 64); // 64KB
            this.lock = new Int32Array(this.sharedBuffer, 0, 1);

            const workerCode = `
                self.onmessage = (e) => {
                    const { url, id, sharedBuffer } = e.data;
                    const lock = new Int32Array(sharedBuffer, 0, 1);
                    const dataView = new Uint8Array(sharedBuffer, 4);
                    
                    try {
                        const xhr = new XMLHttpRequest();
                        xhr.open('GET', url, false);
                        xhr.send();
                        
                        if (xhr.status >= 200 && xhr.status < 300) {
                            const responseText = xhr.responseText;
                            const encoded = new TextEncoder().encode(responseText);
                            
                            if (encoded.length < dataView.length) {
                                dataView.set(encoded);
                                new Int32Array(sharedBuffer, 4, 1)[0] = encoded.length;
                                Atomics.store(lock, 0, id);
                            } else {
                                Atomics.store(lock, 0, -id); // Signal error - too large
                            }
                        } else {
                            Atomics.store(lock, 0, -id);
                        }
                    } catch (err) {
                        Atomics.store(lock, 0, -id);
                    }
                    Atomics.notify(lock, 0);
                };
            `;

            const blob = new Blob([workerCode], { type: 'application/javascript' });
            this.worker = new Worker(URL.createObjectURL(blob));
        } catch (e) {
            console.warn('FX-WARN: Failed to initialize module loader worker.');
        }
    }

    load(url) {
        if (!this.worker || !this.sharedBuffer) {
            throw new Error('Module loader not available. Use async import instead.');
        }

        const id = Date.now() + Math.random();
        this.worker.postMessage({ url, id, sharedBuffer: this.sharedBuffer });

        const result = Atomics.wait(this.lock, 0, 0, 10000);
        
        if (result === 'timed-out') {
            throw new Error(`Module loading timed out: ${url}`);
        }

        const signal = Atomics.load(this.lock, 0);
        if (signal === -id) {
            throw new Error(`Failed to load module: ${url}`);
        }

        const dataLength = new Int32Array(this.sharedBuffer, 4, 1)[0];
        const data = new Uint8Array(this.sharedBuffer, 8, dataLength);
        const moduleCode = new TextDecoder().decode(data);
        
        Atomics.store(this.lock, 0, 0);
        return moduleCode;
    }
}

// ExecutionContext for async management
class ExecutionContext {
    constructor() {
        this.taskQueue = [];
        this.isExecuting = false;
        this.sequences = new Map();
        this.cache = new Map();
        this.maxWaitTime = 5000;
    }

    async waitForAll(timeout = this.maxWaitTime) {
        const startTime = Date.now();
        while (this.taskQueue.length > 0 || this.isExecuting) {
            if (Date.now() - startTime > timeout) {
                throw new Error(`ExecutionContext.waitForAll timed out after ${timeout}ms`);
            }
            await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 10));
        }
    }

    enqueue(task) {
        this.taskQueue.push(task);
        this.executeNext();
    }

    async executeNext() {
        if (this.taskQueue.length === 0 || this.isExecuting) return;
        
        this.isExecuting = true;
        const task = this.taskQueue.shift();
        
        try {
            await task();
        } catch (error) {
            console.error('Task execution error:', error);
        } finally {
            this.isExecuting = false;
            this.executeNext();
        }
    }

    await(promiseOrValue) {
        return Promise.resolve(promiseOrValue).catch(error => {
            console.error('Error in ExecutionContext.await:', error);
            throw error;
        });
    }

    async runAsync(fn, config = {}) {
        const { sq = 0, cache, retry = 0, cb, sqcb } = config;

        if (!this.sequences.has(sq)) {
            this.sequences.set(sq, Promise.resolve());
        }

        const sequence = this.sequences.get(sq).then(async () => {
            const cacheKey = JSON.stringify({ fn: fn.toString(), config });
            
            if (cache && cache !== 1 && this.cache.has(cacheKey)) {
                const { value, timestamp } = this.cache.get(cacheKey);
                if (cache === 0 || Date.now() - timestamp < cache) {
                    return value;
                }
            }

            let result;
            for (let attempt = 0; attempt <= retry; attempt++) {
                try {
                    result = await fn();
                    break;
                } catch (error) {
                    if (attempt === retry) throw error;
                }
            }

            if (cache && cache !== 1) {
                this.cache.set(cacheKey, { value: result, timestamp: Date.now() });
            }

            if (cb) cb(result);
            if (sqcb !== undefined) {
                this.sequences.get(sqcb)?.then(() => {
                    this.runAsync(() => {}, { sq: sqcb });
                });
            }

            return result;
        });

        this.sequences.set(sq, sequence);
        return sequence;
    }
}

// Global registries
const classRegistry = new Map();
const nodeRegistry = new WeakMap();
const pathRegistry = new WeakMap();

// Utility functions
function guessType(val) {
    if (typeof val === 'string') return 'str';
    if (typeof val === 'number') return 'num';
    if (typeof val === 'boolean') return 'bool';
    if (typeof val === 'function') return 'function';
    if (val !== null && typeof val === 'object' && val.constructor === Object) return 'json';
    return 'text';
}

function getNodePath(node) {
    return pathRegistry.get(node) || '';
}

function setNodePath(node, path) {
    pathRegistry.set(node, path);
}

// FXNode creation
function createNode(parentId = null, path = '') {
    const node = {
        // Core properties
        __id: randomUUID(),
        __parent_id: parentId,
        __effects: [],
        __value: {},
        __nodes: {},
        __type: null,
        __proto: null,
        __watchers: new Set(),
        __processes: new Map(),
        __state: 'idle',

        // Core methods
        set(val) {
            const oldValue = this.val();
            let newType = this.__type;

            fx.triggerEffects(this, 'before.set', 'value', val);

            if (typeof val === 'object' && val !== null && val.constructor !== Object) {
                newType = val.constructor.name;
                this.__proto = newType;
                this.__value[newType] = val;
            } else if (typeof val === 'object' && val !== null) {
                newType = 'json';
                for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(val)) {
                    this[k].set(v);
                }
            } else {
                newType = newType || guessType(val);
                this.__value[newType] = val;
            }

            if (!this.__type) {
                this.__type = newType;
            }

            this.notify(oldValue, this.val());
            fx.triggerEffects(this, 'after.set', 'value', val);
            return this;
        },

        val(def) {
            fx.triggerEffects(this, 'before.get', 'value', undefined);
            
            if (!this.__type) {
                fx.triggerEffects(this, 'after.get', 'value', def);
                return def;
            }

            if (this.__type === 'html' && this.__value.dom) {
                const el = this.__value.dom;
                let value;
                if (['INPUT', 'TEXTAREA', 'SELECT'].includes(el.tagName)) {
                    value = el.value;
                } else if (['IMG', 'VIDEO', 'AUDIO', 'SOURCE'].includes(el.tagName)) {
                    value = el.src;
                } else {
                    value = el.innerHTML;
                }
                fx.triggerEffects(this, 'after.get', 'value', value);
                return value;
            }

            const value = this.__value[this.__type];
            const result = value !== undefined ? value : def;
            fx.triggerEffects(this, 'after.get', 'value', result);
            return result;
        },

        type(newType) {
            if (newType === undefined) return this.__type;
            this.__type = newType;
            return this;
        },

        inherit(protoNode) {
            this.__proto = (typeof protoNode === 'string') ? fx.$$(protoNode) : protoNode;
            return this;
        },

        // Reactivity
        watch(callback) {
            this.__watchers.add(callback);
            return () => this.__watchers.delete(callback);
        },

        notify(oldValue, newValue) {
            if (oldValue !== newValue) {
                this.__watchers.forEach(cb => {
                    try {
                        cb(newValue, oldValue, this);
                    } catch (error) {
                        console.error('Error in watcher:', error);
                    }
                });
            }
        },

        // Process management
        flow(name, processFn) {
            this.__processes.set(name, processFn);
            return this;
        },

        async execute(name, ...args) {
            const processFn = this.__processes.get(name);
            if (typeof processFn !== 'function') {
                throw new Error(`Process '${name}' not found.`);
            }
            return await processFn.call(this, ...args);
        },

        select(selector) {
            return fx.queryEngine(this, selector);
        },

        // Serialization
        wrap() {
            const output = { 
                __type: this.__type, 
                __state: this.__state,
                __id: this.__id,
                __parent_id: this.__parent_id
            };
            
            if (typeof this.__proto === 'string' && classRegistry.has(this.__proto)) {
                output.__proto = this.__proto;
                output.__type = 'json';
                const instance = this.val();
                const data = {};
                for (const key in instance) {
                    if (Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(instance, key) && typeof instance[key] !== 'function') {
                        data[key] = instance[key];
                    }
                }
                output.__value = JSON.stringify(data);
            } else if (this.__type === 'html' && this.__value.dom && IS_CLIENT) {
                const el = this.__value.dom;
                output.__value = {
                    tagName: el.tagName,
                    attributes: Array.from(el.attributes).reduce((acc, attr) => ({ ...acc, [attr.name]: attr.value }), {}),
                    innerHTML: el.innerHTML
                };
            } else if (this.__type) {
                output.__value = this.val();
            }

            if (Object.keys(this.__nodes).length > 0) {
                output.__nodes = {};
                for (const [key, childNode] of Object.entries(this.__nodes)) {
                    output.__nodes[key] = childNode.wrap();
                }
            }
            
            return output;
        },

        expand(data) {
            this.__type = data.__type;
            this.__state = data.__state || 'idle';
            this.__proto = data.__proto || null;
            this.__id = data.__id || this.__id;
            this.__parent_id = data.__parent_id || this.__parent_id;

            if (data.__proto && classRegistry.has(data.__proto)) {
                const RegisteredClass = classRegistry.get(data.__proto);
                const instance = new RegisteredClass();
                const jsonData = (typeof data.__value === 'string') ? JSON.parse(data.__value) : data.__value;
                Object.assign(instance, jsonData);
                this.set(instance);
            } else if (data.__type === 'html' && IS_CLIENT) {
                const { tagName, attributes, innerHTML } = data.__value;
                const el = document.createElement(tagName);
                for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(attributes)) {
                    el.setAttribute(key, value);
                }
                el.innerHTML = innerHTML;
                this.__value.dom = el;
                this.set({
                    tag: el.tagName.toLowerCase(),
                    id: el.id,
                    class: el.className,
                    text: el.innerText
                });
            } else if (data.__value !== undefined) {
                this.set(data.__value);
            }

            if (data.__nodes) {
                for (const [key, childData] of Object.entries(data.__nodes)) {
                    this[key].expand(childData);
                }
            }
            
            return this;
        }
    };

    setNodePath(node, path);
    return new Proxy(node, nodeHandler);
}

// Node proxy handler
const nodeHandler = {
    get(target, key) {
        if (typeof key === 'symbol' || key === 'then') return undefined;
        if (key in target) return target[key];

        // Trigger effects for property access
        fx.triggerEffects(target, 'before.get', key, undefined);

        const value = target.val();
        if (typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && key in value) {
            const prop = value[key];
            const result = typeof prop === 'function' ? prop.bind(value) : prop;
            fx.triggerEffects(target, 'after.get', key, result);
            return result;
        }

        // Check prototype chain
        let proto = target.__proto;
        if (proto && typeof proto !== 'string') {
            while (proto) {
                if (proto.__nodes && key in proto.__nodes) {
                    const result = proto.__nodes[key];
                    fx.triggerEffects(target, 'after.get', key, result);
                    return result;
                }
                proto = proto.__proto;
            }
        }

        // Create new node if it doesn't exist
        if (!(key in target.__nodes)) {
            const currentPath = getNodePath(target);
            const newPath = currentPath ? `${currentPath}.${key}` : key;
            target.__nodes[key] = createNode(target.__id, newPath);
        }

        const result = target.__nodes[key];
        fx.triggerEffects(target, 'after.get', key, result);
        return result;
    },

    set(target, key, val) {
        if (key in target || typeof key === 'symbol') {
            target[key] = val;
        } else {
            fx.triggerEffects(target, 'before.set', key, val);
            const currentPath = getNodePath(target);
            const newPath = currentPath ? `${currentPath}.${key}` : key;
            target.__nodes[key] = createNode(target.__id, newPath).set(val);
            fx.triggerEffects(target, 'after.set', key, val);
        }
        return true;
    }
};

// Resource factory
function createResourceFactory() {
    return async function resourceFactory(type) {
        // const resourceMap = {
        //     'api': () => import('./fx/APIResource.js'),
        //     'css': () => import('./fx/CSSResource.js'),
        //     'html': () => import('./fx/HTMLResource.js'),
        //     'module': () => import('./fx/ModuleResource.js'),
        //     'json': () => import('./fx/DataResource.js'),
        //     'raw': () => import('./fx/RawResource.js')
        // };

        if (resourceMap[type]) {
            const module = await resourceMap[type]();
            return module.default;
        }
        
        console.error(`Unknown resource type: ${type}`);
        return null;
    };
}

// Query engine for CSS-like selectors
function queryEngine(startNode, selector) {
    const parts = selector.split(/\s+/);
    let currentNodes = [startNode];

    for (const part of parts) {
        const nextNodes = new Set();
        const attrMatch = part.match(/\[\s*([_a-zA-Z0-9]+)\s*(=|!=)\s*"([^"]+)"\s*\]/);
        const nameMatch = part.match(/^([a-zA-Z0-9*_-]+)/);

        for (const node of currentNodes) {
            const children = Object.values(node.__nodes);
            for (const child of children) {
                let matches = true;
                
                if (nameMatch && nameMatch[1] !== '*') {
                    const childPath = getNodePath(child);
                    if (!childPath.endsWith(nameMatch[1])) {
                        matches = false;
                    }
                }
                
                if (attrMatch) {
                    const [_, attr, op, value] = attrMatch;
                    const nodeValue = (typeof child[attr] === 'function') ? child[attr]() : child[attr];
                    if (op === '=' && String(nodeValue) !== value) matches = false;
                    if (op === '!=' && String(nodeValue) === value) matches = false;
                }
                
                if (matches) {
                    nextNodes.add(child);
                }
            }
        }
        currentNodes = Array.from(nextNodes);
    }
    return currentNodes;
}

// Main FX class
class FX {
    static #instance = null;

    constructor() {
        if (FX.#instance) {
            throw new Error("FX is a singleton. Use FX.getInstance() instead.");
        }

        this.root = createNode('root', '');
        this.context = new ExecutionContext();
        this.resourceFactory = createResourceFactory();
        this.moduleLoader = IS_CLIENT ? new ClientModuleLoader() : null;
        this.manifestObj = {};
        this.resources = {};
        this.debug = false;

        return new Proxy(this, {
            get: (target, prop, receiver) => this.handleGet(target, prop, receiver),
            apply: (target, thisArg, args) => this.$$(args[0], args[1])
        });
    }

    static getInstance() {
        if (!FX.#instance) {
            FX.#instance = new FX();
        }
        return FX.#instance;
    }

    handleGet(target, prop, receiver) {
        // Core FX methods and properties
        if (prop in target) {
            return Reflect.get(target, prop, receiver);
        }

        // Delegate to root node for dynamic properties
        return target.root[prop];
    }

    // Effects system
    triggerEffects(node, event, key, value) {
        // Execute effects on current node
        for (const effect of node.__effects) {
            try {
                effect(event, node, key, value);
            } catch (error) {
                console.error(`Effect error on ${event}:`, error);
            }
        }

        // Execute effects from prototype chain
        if (node.__proto && typeof node.__proto !== 'string') {
            this.triggerEffects(node.__proto, event, key, value);
        }
    }

    // Main accessor function
    $$(path, value) {
        if (!path) return this.root;

        let logicalPath = path;
        let manifestPath = null;
        
        if (path.includes('@')) {
            [logicalPath, manifestPath] = path.split('@');
        }

        const keys = logicalPath.split('.');
        let current = this.root;
        
        for (const key of keys) {
            if (!key) continue;
            current = current[key];
        }

        // Handle manifest-based loading
        if (manifestPath && current.__type === null) {
            this.handleManifestLoading(current, manifestPath);
        }

        if (value !== undefined) {
            if (Array.isArray(value)) {
                const fn = current.val();
                return typeof fn === 'function' ? fn(...value) : undefined;
            }
            current.set(value);
        }

        return current;
    }

    handleManifestLoading(node, manifestPath) {
        const manifestNode = this.$$(`__modules.${manifestPath}`).val();
        if (manifestNode) {
            const { location, export: exportKey, type: className } = manifestNode;
            
            let moduleInstance;
            if (IS_SERVER) {
                try {
                    const module = requireSync(location);
                    const ExportedThing = module[exportKey];
                    moduleInstance = new ExportedThing();
                } catch (error) {
                    console.error('Server-side module loading failed:', error);
                    return;
                }
            } else {
                if (this.moduleLoader) {
                    try {
                        this.moduleLoader.load(location);
                        const RegisteredClass = classRegistry.get(className);
                        if (RegisteredClass) {
                            moduleInstance = new RegisteredClass();
                        } else {
                            throw new Error(`Client module '${className}' not found in registry.`);
                        }
                    } catch (error) {
                        console.error('Client-side module loading failed:', error);
                        return;
                    }
                }
            }
            
            if (moduleInstance) {
                node.set(moduleInstance);
            }
        }
    }

    // DOM integration
    $(selector) {
        if (!IS_CLIENT) {
            console.warn("DOM selector '$' called in non-browser environment.");
            return null;
        }

        const elements = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll(selector));
        if (elements.length === 0) return null;

        const wrapElement = (el) => {
            const node = createNode();
            node.type('html');
            node.__value.dom = el;
            node.set({
                tag: el.tagName.toLowerCase(),
                id: el.id,
                class: el.className,
                text: el.innerText
            });
            return node;
        };

        const nodes = elements.map(wrapElement);
        return nodes.length === 1 ? nodes[0] : nodes;
    }

    // CSS-like querying
    $$$(selector) {
        return queryEngine(this.root, selector);
    }

    // Utility methods
    register(classObj, name) {
        classRegistry.set(name || classObj.name, classObj);
        return this;
    }

    createNode(parentId, path) {
        return createNode(parentId, path);
    }

    select(selector) {
        return this.$$$(selector);
    }

    query(selector) {
        return this.$$$(selector);
    }

    // Serialization
    wrap() {
        return this.root.wrap();
    }

    expand(data) {
        return this.root.expand(data);
    }

    // Configuration
    fx(config = {}) {
        const processedConfig = this.processConfig(config);
        
        return new Proxy(this, {
            get: (target, prop) => {
                if (prop in target) {
                    const value = target[prop];
                    if (typeof value === 'function') {
                        return (...args) => this.context.runAsync(() => value.apply(target, args), processedConfig);
                    }
                    return value;
                }
                return target.root[prop];
            }
        });
    }

    processConfig(config) {
        if (typeof config !== 'object' || config === null) {
            config = { sq: config };
        }
        return {
            sq: config.sq ?? 0,
            retry: config.retry ?? 0,
            cache: config.cache ?? 1,
            cb: config.cb,
            sqcb: config.sqcb,
            ...config
        };
    }

    // Manifest loading
    async loadManifest(manifestObj, prefix = '') {
        for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(manifestObj)) {
            const fullKey = prefix ? `${prefix}.${key}` : key;
            
            if (typeof value === 'function') {
                const loadedValue = await this.context.await(value());
                await this.loadManifest(loadedValue, fullKey);
            } else if (typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && !value.type) {
                let current = this.manifestObj;
                const parts = fullKey.split('.');
                for (let i = 0; i < parts.length; i++) {
                    const part = parts[i];
                    if (i === parts.length - 1) {
                        current[part] = value;
                    } else {
                        current[part] = current[part] || {};
                        current = current[part];
                    }
                }
                await this.loadManifest(value, fullKey);
            } else {
                let current = this.manifestObj;
                const parts = fullKey.split('.');
                for (let i = 0; i < parts.length; i++) {
                    const part = parts[i];
                    if (i === parts.length - 1) {
                        current[part] = value;
                    } else {
                        current[part] = current[part] || {};
                        current = current[part];
                    }
                }
                
                const resource = await this.createResource(value, fullKey);
                if (resource !== null) {
                    this.resources[fullKey] = resource;
                }
            }
        }
    }

    async createResource(config, fullKey) {
        if (typeof config !== 'object' || config === null || !config.type) {
            return null;
        }

        try {
            const ResourceClass = await this.resourceFactory(config.type);
            if (!ResourceClass) {
                console.error(`Resource class not found for type: ${config.type}`);
                return null;
            }
            return new ResourceClass(config, this.context);
        } catch (error) {
            console.error(`Error creating resource for ${fullKey}:`, error);
            return null;
        }
    }

    // Helper methods
    set(key, value) {
        return this.$$(key, value);
    }

    get(key, defaultValue) {
        const node = this.$$(key);
        return node ? node.val(defaultValue) : defaultValue;
    }

    data(key, value) {
        if (arguments.length === 1) {
            return this.get(key);
        }
        return this.set(key, value);
    }

    async waitForAll(timeout) {
        return this.context.waitForAll(timeout);
    }
}

// Helper function for adding effects
function $effect(node, effectFunction) {
    node.__effects.push(effectFunction);
    return node;
}

// Create and export the FX instance
const fx = FX.getInstance();

// Global exports (optional - can be removed if not desired)
if (typeof globalThis !== 'undefined') {
    globalThis.$$ = fx.$$;
    globalThis.$ = fx.$;
    globalThis.$$$ = fx.$$$;
    globalThis.$effect = $effect;
}

export { fx, FX, $effect };
export default fx;
```


### `core\fx.md`

```md
This production-ready FX framework combines all the best features from both implementations plus the effects system:

## Key Features Implemented:

### 1. **Dynamic Node Creation**
- `fx.user.profile.name = "John"` creates nodes on-the-fly
- Proxy-based property access throughout

### 2. **Effects System** (New)
- `before.get`, `after.get`, `before.set`, `after.set` events
- Effects propagate through prototype chain
- Use `$effect(node, effectFunction)` to add effects

### 3. **Isomorphic Module Loading**
- SharedArrayBuffer + Worker for client-side sync-like loading
- Server-side sync require for Node.js
- `fx.$$('instance@module.path')` syntax

### 4. **Serialization/Deserialization**
- `fx.wrap()` - serialize entire state
- `fx.expand(data)` - restore from serialized state
- Handles class instances, DOM elements, complex objects

### 5. **ExecutionContext**
- Async management with sync-like API
- Sequences, retries, caching, callbacks
- `fx({ retry: 3, cache: 5000 }).api.users.get()`

### 6. **DOM Integration**
- `fx.$('div.container')` - jQuery-style selectors
- DOM elements wrapped in FX nodes

### 7. **CSS-like Querying**
- `fx.$$$('[__type="User"]')` - query internal node structure
- Advanced selectors for finding nodes

### 8. **Reactivity**
- `node.watch(callback)` for reactive behaviors
- Automatic change notifications

### 9. **Resource Management**
- Factory pattern for different resource types
- Manifest-based configuration
- Lazy loading support

## Usage Examples:

```javascript
// Dynamic node creation
fx.user.profile.name = "John";

// Effects system
$effect(fx.user, (event, node, key, value) => {
    if (event === 'after.set') {
        console.log(`User ${key} changed to:`, value);
    }
});

// Module loading with sync-like syntax
const userService = fx.$$('userService@modules/UserService');
userService.createUser({ name: "John", email: "john@example.com" });

// API calls with configuration
const users = fx({ retry: 3, cache: 5000 }).api.users.get('/users');

// Reactivity
fx.user.name.watch((newValue, oldValue) => {
   console.log(`Name changed from ${oldValue} to ${newValue}`);
});

// Serialization
const appState = fx.wrap();
localStorage.setItem('appState', JSON.stringify(appState));

// Restoration
const savedState = JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem('appState'));
fx.expand(savedState);

// DOM integration
const button = fx.$('button.submit');
button.text = "Click me!";

// Querying nodes
const allUsers = fx.$$$('[__type="User"]');
const activeUsers = fx.$$$('[status="active"]');

// Process flows
fx.user.flow('validate', function(data) {
   if (!data.email) throw new Error('Email required');
   return data;
});

await fx.user.execute('validate', { name: "John" });
```
## Effects System Examples:

```js
// Database synchronization effect
function dbSyncEffect(event, node, key, value) {
    if (event === 'after.set') {
        const path = getNodePath(node);
        database.save(`${path}.${key}`, value);
    }
    
    if (event === 'before.get' && !node.__nodes[key]) {
        const path = getNodePath(node);
        const dbValue = database.load(`${path}.${key}`);
        if (dbValue !== null) {
            node.__nodes[key] = fx.createNode(node.__id).set(dbValue);
        }
    }
}

// Validation effect
function validationEffect(event, node, key, value) {
    if (event === 'before.set') {
        if (key === 'email' && !isValidEmail(value)) {
            throw new Error('Invalid email format');
        }
    }
}

// Real-time sync effect
function realtimeSyncEffect(event, node, key, value) {
    if (event === 'after.set') {
        websocket.broadcast({
            path: `${getNodePath(node)}.${key}`,
            value: value,
            timestamp: Date.now()
        });
    }
}

// Apply effects to nodes
$effect(fx.user, dbSyncEffect);
$effect(fx.user, validationEffect);
$effect(fx.app, realtimeSyncEffect);
```


## Advanced Features:
### 1. Prototype Inheritance

```js
// Create a prototype node
fx.templates.user.set({
    name: "",
    email: "",
    validate() { return this.email.includes('@'); }
});

// Inherit from prototype
fx.users.john.inherit('templates.user');
fx.users.john.name = "John";
fx.users.john.validate(); // Inherited method
```


### 2. Complex Workflows

```js
// Define a complex user creation workflow
fx.workflows.createUser.flow('validate', function(userData) {
    // Validation logic
    return userData;
}).flow('save', function(userData) {
    // Save to database
    return this.database.users.create(userData);
}).flow('notify', function(user) {
    // Send notification
    this.notifications.send(user.email, 'Welcome!');
    return user;
});

// Execute workflow
const newUser = await fx.workflows.createUser
    .execute('validate', { name: "John", email: "john@example.com" })
    .then(() => fx.workflows.createUser.execute('save'))
    .then(() => fx.workflows.createUser.execute('notify'));
```

### 3. Environment-Specific Features

```js
// Server-side only
if (IS_SERVER) {
    // File system access
    fx.files.config = fs.readFileSync('./config.json', 'utf8');
    
    // Database connections
    fx.db.connection = await mongoose.connect(process.env.MONGODB_URI);
}

// Client-side only
if (IS_CLIENT) {
    // Local storage integration
    fx.storage.user = JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem('user') || '{}');
    
    // DOM manipulation
    fx.$('body').addClass('app-loaded');
}
```

## Production Considerations:
### 1. Error Handling 

```js
// Global error effect
function errorHandlingEffect(event, node, key, value) {
    try {
        // Normal processing
    } catch (error) {
        console.error(`Error in ${event} on ${getNodePath(node)}.${key}:`, error);
        // Log to error reporting service
        errorReporter.log(error, { event, node: getNodePath(node), key, value });
    }
}

$effect(fx, errorHandlingEffect);
```

### 2. Performance Monitoring
```js
function performanceEffect(event, node, key, value) {
    const start = performance.now();
    
    return function cleanup() {
        const duration = performance.now() - start;
        if (duration > 100) { // Log slow operations
            console.warn(`Slow operation: ${event} on ${getNodePath(node)}.${key} took ${duration}ms`);
        }
    };
}
```

### 3. Security Considerations
```js
function securityEffect(event, node, key, value) {
    if (event === 'before.set') {
        // Sanitize input
        if (typeof value === 'string') {
            value = sanitizeInput(value);
        }
        
        // Check permissions
        const userRole = fx.auth.currentUser.role;
        if (!hasPermission(userRole, getNodePath(node), key)) {
            throw new Error('Access denied');
        }
    }
}
```


## Testing Integration:
```js
// Test helper for effects
function createTestEffect() {
    const calls = [];
    const effect = (event, node, key, value) => {
        calls.push({ event, node: getNodePath(node), key, value });
    };
    effect.getCalls = () => calls;
    effect.reset = () => calls.length = 0;
    return effect;
}

// Usage in tests
const testEffect = createTestEffect();
$effect(fx.user, testEffect);

fx.user.name = "John";
expect(testEffect.getCalls()).toContainEqual({
    event: 'after.set',
    node: 'user',
    key: 'name',
    value: 'John'
});
```
```


### `docs\design.md`

```md
# Digital Cabinet Relay - Comprehensive Design Document

## 1. Overview

The DCR is a fullstack, extensible orchestration engine designed to:

* Import Postman collections and expose them as modular, managed endpoints
* Integrate and communicate with arbitrary sub-APIs (microservices) via HTTP, WebSocket (WS), and RPC
* Apply a declarative lifecycle execution model to each request and DB operation
* Store configuration, permission, and user data in a persistent database
* Log all activities, errors, and metadata to SQLite, with scheduled export to S3
* Visualize runtime and flow graphs via a D3-based dashboard frontend
* Support runtime configuration overrides, caching, retries, auth, and webhooks at every phase

The system is designed for **runtime flexibility**, **multi-tech interoperability**, and **developer observability**.

---

## 2. Technology Stack

| Layer         | Technology                      | Notes                                         |
| ------------- | ------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
| Web Framework | Hono (Node.js + TypeScript)     | Fast, ESM-friendly web server with WS support |
| ORM           | Drizzle ORM                     | Type-safe, SQLite & Postgres compatible       |
| DB (runtime)  | SQLite (local), Postgres (prod) | Logs + Users + Endpoint definitions           |
| Validation    | Zod                             | Shared schema between frontend/backend        |
| Config        | `.env` + hierarchical `.json`   | Global + API-level + Endpoint-level overrides |
| Frontend      | Vite + React + ShadCN           | Dashboard, dark/light mode, D3 visualizer     |
| Visualization | D3.js                           | Flow diagram of request lifecycles            |
| State Sharing | WebSocket, HTTP, RPC (custom)   | For real-time communication and fallback      |
| Logging       | Configurable (SQLite, PG, MySQL, File) + Archival (S3, FTP, Local) | Driver-based, typed, with lifecycle & archival |
| Auth          | JWT / API key / per-endpoint    | Pluggable in lifecycle hooks                  |

---

## 3. `.env` Structure

```ini
# Main Database
DATABASE_URL="sqlite:///./data/dcr.db"

# Orchestrator Mode
ORCHESTRATOR_MODE=dev # dev, prod, test

# AWS Credentials (used for S3 log archiving and potentially other AWS services)
AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID=your_aws_access_key_id
AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=your_aws_secret_access_key
S3_REGION=your_s3_region # e.g., us-east-1

# Log Archiving & Storage Configuration
LOG_STORAGE_DRIVER=s3 # s3, ftp, archive
LOG_STORAGE_INTERVAL=7 # In days, how long to keep logs locally before archiving
LOG_STORAGE_ZIP_OPTION=zipPerLog # zipPerLog, zipAllTogether, none

# S3 Specific Log Storage
S3_BUCKET_NAME=your_dcr_archived_logs_bucket
S3_STORAGE_PREFIX=logs/production/ # Optional prefix within the bucket

# FTP Specific Log Storage
FTP_HOST=your_ftp_host
FTP_PORT=21
FTP_USER=your_ftp_user
FTP_PASSWORD=your_ftp_password
FTP_REMOTE_PATH=/dcr_archived_logs/

# Archive (Local Filesystem) Specific Log Storage
LOG_STORAGE_ARCHIVE_PATH=/mnt/dcr_archived_logs/
```

---

## 4. Config Hierarchy

### 4.1 Orchestrator Config (`config/orchestrator.json`)

```json
{
  "logLevel": "debug",
  "defaultRetries": 3,
  "defaultCacheTtl": 300,
  "lifecycle": {
    "beforeRequest": ["auth", "cache", "log"],
    "request": ["fetch"],
    "afterRequest": ["log", "webhook"]
  }
}
```

### 4.2 API Config (`apis/salesforce/config.json`)

```json
{
  "auth": {
    "type": "oauth2",
    "tokenUrl": "https://login.salesforce.com/oauth2/token"
  },
  "baseUrl": "https://api.salesforce.com/v1"
}
```

### 4.3 Endpoint Config (`apis/salesforce/endpoints/getUsers.json`)

### 4.4 Logging and Archiving Strategy

The DCR employs a flexible and configurable logging system for both main orchestrator/API operations and for archiving logs to long-term storage.

#### 4.4.1 Main Logging System

The primary logging system is designed to be adaptable to different database backends and file-based outputs.

*   **Log Drivers**: Each defined log type can specify a driver. Supported drivers include:
    *   `mysql`: For logging to a MySQL database.
    *   `postgresql`: For logging to a PostgreSQL database.
    *   `sqlite`: Default for local/runtime logs before archiving (uses the main `DATABASE_URL` or a separate one).
    *   `file`: For simple file-based logging (e.g., rotating log files).
*   **Log Classification/Types**: Logs will be categorized (e.g., `application`, `security`, `request_lifecycle`, `database_query`, `external_api_call`) to allow for granular configuration, filtering, and routing to different drivers or storage.
*   **Configuration**:
    *   Main logging configuration (default driver, log levels per type, driver-specific settings) will reside in `config/orchestrator.json`.
    *   This configuration is inherited by sub-APIs, which can override parts of it in their respective API-level or endpoint-level `config.json` files.
    *   Example `orchestrator.json` addition for logging:
        ```json
        {
          // ... existing orchestrator config (logLevel, defaultRetries, etc.) ...
          "logging": {
            "defaultDriver": "sqlite", 
            "logTypes": {
              "application": { "driver": "file", "level": "info" },
              "request_lifecycle": { "driver": "sqlite", "level": "debug" },
              "security": { "driver": "mysql", "level": "warn" }
            },
            "drivers": {
              "sqlite": {
                // Uses main DATABASE_URL by default if not specified
                // "connectionString": "sqlite:///./data/dcr_audit_logs.db" 
              },
              "file": {
                "basePath": "./logs/", // Base directory for log files
                "maxSizeMB": 10,      // Max size per log file before rotation
                "maxFiles": 7         // Number of rotated files to keep
              },
              "mysql": {
                "connectionString": "mysql://user:pass@host:port/dbname_logs"
              },
              "postgresql": {
                "connectionString": "postgresql://user:pass@host:port/dbname_logs"
              }
            }
          }
        }
        ```

#### 4.4.2 Log Archiving and Long-Term Storage

Logs, especially those initially stored in transient systems like SQLite or local files, will be periodically archived to a more permanent storage location based on their age.

*   **Storage Drivers**: Configured via `LOG_STORAGE_DRIVER` environment variable or within the orchestrator config (e.g., `logging.archival.driver`).
    *   `s3`: Amazon S3.
    *   `ftp`: FTP/SFTP server.
    *   `archive`: Local filesystem archive (moving files to a different directory).
*   **Archival Interval**: Configured via `LOG_STORAGE_INTERVAL` (e.g., `7` for 7 days). Logs older than this interval are targeted for archiving.
*   **Compression Options**: Configured via `LOG_STORAGE_ZIP_OPTION`.
    *   `zipPerLog`: Each individual log source/file (e.g., a daily rotated file, or logs for a specific endpoint if stored separately) is zipped.
    *   `zipAllTogether`: All logs qualifying for an archival run are consolidated into a single zip archive.
    *   `none`: Logs are transferred/moved without compression.
*   **Driver-Specific Configuration**: Primarily managed via `.env` variables (as shown in Section 3) and potentially referenced or overridden in the orchestrator config.
    *   **S3**: Requires `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `S3_BUCKET_NAME`, `S3_REGION`, and an optional `S3_STORAGE_PREFIX`.
    *   **FTP**: Requires `FTP_HOST`, `FTP_PORT`, `FTP_USER`, `FTP_PASSWORD`, and `FTP_REMOTE_PATH`.
    *   **Archive (Local Filesystem)**: Requires `LOG_STORAGE_ARCHIVE_PATH`.

```json
{
  "method": "GET",
  "path": "/users",
  "authOverride": "none",
  "cacheTtl": 600,
  "lifecycle": {
    "beforeRequest": ["cache", "log"],
    "request": ["http"],
    "afterRequest": ["log", "webhook"]
  },
  "webhooks": {
    "onSuccess": "https://hooks.site/ok",
    "onError": "https://hooks.site/fail"
  }
}
```

---

## 5. Database Schema (Drizzle ORM)

### Tables

* `users`: { id, email, passwordHash, role }
* `permissions`: { userId, apiName, endpointPath, verb, allowed }
* `apis`: { id, name, baseUrl, type, configJson }
* `endpoints`: { id, apiId, path, method, configJson }
* `logs`: { id, timestamp, endpointId, phase, success, message, metadataJson }
* `webhooks`: { id, endpointId, phase, statusCode, url }
* `cache`: { key, valueJson, expiresAt }
* `retry_schedule`: { requestId, endpointId, nextTry, attempts, status }

---

## 6. Lifecycle Execution Model

### Request Lifecycle

1. `beforeRequest`

   * Auth check (JWT, API key, OAuth2)
   * Cache lookup
   * Log intent
2. `request`

   * HTTP request
   * WS message
   * Local RPC call
3. `afterRequest`

   * Log response
   * Webhook emission (by response code)
   * Cache update (if applicable)

### ORM Lifecycle

1. `beforeExecute`

   * Input validation
   * Cache read
2. `execute`

   * Drizzle query or raw SQL
3. `afterExecute`

   * Log result
   * Webhook (success/failure)
   * Retry trigger (if enabled)

---

## 7. Logging

* All phases (`beforeRequest`, `afterExecute`, etc.) log to SQLite
* Log format:

```ts
{
  id: number,
  timestamp: string,
  endpointId: number,
  phase: 'beforeRequest' | 'execute' | 'afterExecute',
  success: boolean,
  message: string,
  metadataJson: object
}
```

* Export to S3 every 24h:

  * Cron job runs daily
  * Uploads logs from exactly 7 days ago

---

## 8. Sub-API / Postman Mapping

### Supported Input:

* Postman v2.1 collections
* OpenAPI 3.1 (future)
* Manual `.json` config for custom sub-APIs

### Mapping:

| Postman Element | AOC Equivalent           |
| --------------- | ------------------------ |
| Collection      | API registration         |
| Request URL     | Endpoint path + method   |
| Auth            | Lifecycle `auth`         |
| Tests / Scripts | Lifecycle `afterRequest` |

---

## 9. Communication Protocols

| Protocol  | Use                                   | Fallback                   |
| --------- | ------------------------------------- | -------------------------- |
| HTTP      | RESTful endpoints                     | Retry, failover via config |
| WebSocket | Live dashboard feed, long-running ops | Auto-reconnect             |
| RPC       | Local sub-api exec                    | HTTP fallback              |

All protocols are routed through lifecycle hooks and use shared logging.

---

## 10. Retry & Scheduling

Each request config can include:

```json
"retry": {
  "count": 3,
  "backoff": "exponential",
  "initialDelay": 1000
}
```

If request fails, it’s queued in `retry_schedule` with timestamped `nextTry`.

---

## 11. Dashboard UI (React + ShadCN)

### Pages

* Dashboard: Live D3 visualization of endpoints
* Logs: Table viewer with filters
* Users: Admin panel with permission editor
* Endpoints: Config + lifecycle management
* Sub-API Registry: Add/edit APIs + upload Postman
* Webhooks: Attach URL per phase & status code

### Features

* Dark/light mode toggle (ShadCN + Tailwind)
* D3 graph nodes per endpoint + lifecycle stage
* Pinning of logs + cache hits to graph points
* WebSocket-based live updates
* Manual trigger of retries

---

## 12. Summary

The AOC system provides a complete backend orchestration and visualization suite for managing APIs, Postman imports, lifecycle processing, retries, logs, and more. It combines speed (Hono), safety (Zod + Drizzle), extensibility (sub-APIs), and observability (D3 dashboard), and is deployable on AWS EC2 with full config-driven runtime behavior.
```


### `docs\fx.md`

```md
# FX Runtime - A Comprehensive Guide

## 1. Overview

**FX** is a dynamic, isomorphic, and composable runtime object that serves as the core data structure and execution context for the Digital Cabinet Relay (DCR) system. It is globally accessible via `$$` or `fx`.

The fundamental unit of the FX system is a **node**. Everything is a node, forming a hierarchical tree structure similar to a file system or the DOM. This design allows for unparalleled flexibility, introspection, and interoperability.

### Core Principles

*   **Everything is a Node:** From a simple string value to a complex API request context, every piece of data is an `fx` node.
*   **Dynamic by Default:** Nodes and paths are created on-the-fly as they are accessed. `$$('a.b.c')` automatically creates the `a`, `b`, and `c` nodes if they don't exist.
*   **Reactivity:** Nodes can be "watched." Any change to a node's value will notify all of its watchers, enabling real-time updates and reactive programming patterns.
*   **Isomorphism:** `fx.js` is designed to run in both Node.js (backend) and the browser (frontend), with environment-specific features handled gracefully.
*   **Data-Defined Execution:** `fx` nodes can describe a process (a "Process Definition Graph"). A separate service, the `ProcessRunner`, can execute this graph, making the system's logic fully dynamic and configurable.

---

## 2. Global Accessors

The FX system is exposed through three global accessors:

### `$$` (The Main Accessor)

This is the primary way to interact with the FX tree.

*   **Get a node:** `const userNode = $$('data.users.user1');`
*   **Set a value:** `$$('data.users.user1.name', 'Alice');`
*   **Get a value:** `const name = $$('data.users.user1.name').val(); // "Alice"`
*   **Call a function on a node:** `$$('utils.myFunction', [arg1, arg2]);`

### `$` (DOM Selector - Client-Side Only)

Acts like a jQuery selector, finding DOM elements and wrapping them in reactive `fx` nodes.

*   **Select an element:** `const inputNode = $('input#username');`
*   **Two-Way Binding:**
    *   Get DOM value: `const currentValue = inputNode.val();`
    *   Set DOM value: `inputNode.set('new-username');` (This updates the `<input>` element's value).
    *   DOM events on the input will automatically update `inputNode`.

### `$$$` (Graph Query Selector)

Queries the internal `fx` node graph using a CSS-attribute-like syntax.

*   **Find all nodes of a certain type:** `const allUserNodes = $$$('[__type="User"]');`
*   **Find all nodes in a failed state:** `const failedSteps = $$$('[__state="failed"]');`

---

## 3. The `fx` Node API

When you get a node (e.g., `const myNode = $$('path')`), it has several built-in methods:

### `.set(value)`

Sets the value of the node. The node intelligently guesses the type (`str`, `num`, `json`, class instance).
```javascript
$$('config.retries').set(3); // __type becomes 'num'
$$('user.data').set({ name: 'Bob' }); // __type becomes 'json', sub-nodes 'name' created
```

### `.val(defaultValue)`

Retrieves the primary value of the node, based on its `__type`.
```javascript
const retries = $$('config.retries').val(); // Returns the number 3
```

### `.type(newType)`

Gets or sets the primary type of the node.
```javascript
$$('config.timeout').type('str'); // The node will now return its value as a string.
```

### `.watch(callback)`

Registers a callback function to be executed whenever the node's value changes. The callback receives `(newValue, oldValue, node)`. Returns an `unwatch` function.
```javascript
const unwatch = $$('status').watch((newStatus) => {
    console.log(`Status changed to: ${newStatus}`);
});
// To stop listening:
unwatch();
```

### `.flow(name, processFn)` & `.execute(name, ...args)`

Defines and executes a named process on the node. The function is bound to the node (`this` refers to the node).
```javascript
$$('greeter').flow('sayHello', function(name) {
    this.message.set(`Hello, ${name}`);
});
await $$('greeter').execute('sayHello', 'World');
```

### `.wrap()` & `.expand(data)`

Handles serialization and hydration.

*   **.wrap():** Converts the entire `fx` node tree (from the current node down) into a clean, serializable JSON object. This is used for saving to the database.
*   **.expand(data):** Takes a JSON object (the output of `.wrap()`) and reconstructs the `fx` node tree from it, including instantiating any registered classes. This is used to hydrate a node from a saved definition.

### `.inherit(protoNode)`

Sets up prototypal inheritance from another `fx` node. If a property is not found on the current node, the lookup will continue up the prototype chain.

```javascript
const baseConfig = $$('config.base');
baseConfig.timeout.set(5000);

const userApiConfig = $$('config.userApi').inherit(baseConfig);
console.log(userApiConfig.timeout.val()); // 5000
```

---

## 4. Isomorphic Module & Class Loading

FX can load and instantiate classes, making it a powerful tool for dependency injection and plugins.

1.  **Register a Class:** Make a class known to the FX system.
    ```javascript
    class JwtVerifier { /* ... */ }
    fx.register(JwtVerifier); // Registers with name "JwtVerifier"
    ```

2.  **Define it in a Manifest:** Create a node that describes where to find the module.
    ```javascript
    $$('__modules.jwt', {
        location: './plugins/auth/jwt.js', // Path for require()
        export: 'JwtVerifier',             // The exported name
        type: 'JwtVerifier'                // The registered class name
    });
    ```

3.  **Instantiate Dynamically:** Use the `@` syntax to create an instance.
    ```javascript
    const myVerifier = $$('verifiers.prod@jwt');
    // `myVerifier` is now an instance of the JwtVerifier class.
    ```

This works on both the server (using a synchronous `require` helper) and the client (using a blocking Web Worker), providing a unified way to manage dependencies.
```
```


### `docs\notes.md`

```md
Leave this here: This file is for the coding assistant to make notes about what it learned from the previous set of tasks performed. This does not have to be specific to this code base, but anything that would help improve coding performance in the future.
---
# Notes for Coding assistant.

## Lessons Learned: Large Files, Duplicates, and Workflow Improvements

### 1. Handling Large Files and Duplicate Methods
- When working with large files (over 300 lines), only a segment (e.g., 200 lines) is visible at a time. This can cause a lack of complete context and may lead to accidentally duplicating methods or code blocks when making iterative edits.
- In the recent iteration, this led to multiple duplicate function implementations in the `NamespaceService` class, causing TypeScript errors and confusion.
- **Lesson:** For files larger than 300 lines, always proactively recommend or perform splitting into smaller, manageable modules before making any edits. This ensures complete context, avoids duplication, and improves maintainability.

### 2. Improved Coding Workflow for the Assistant
- After each coding run or task:
  1. **Open and review `docs/tasks.md`** to check what is next and to follow any provided context links.
  2. **Add new notes to `docs/notes.md`** about what was learned, challenges, or workflow improvements.
  3. **Re-read previous notes** in `docs/notes.md` to reinforce best practices and avoid repeating past mistakes.
- This workflow will help maintain context, improve coding performance, and ensure continuous improvement.

---
```

### `docs\tasks\seciton12.md`

```md
## 12. Domain-Based Namespace Routing

- [ ] 12.1 Implement `domain -> namespace` resolution from Host header**
    - `backend/src/index.ts`**: **Why?** This is where global middleware is applied. A new middleware function will be added here to intercept requests, read the `Host` header, and resolve it to a namespace. This namespace information would then be added to the Hono context (`c.set('namespace', resolvedNamespace)`).
    - New Backend Service (e.g., `backend/src/services/namespace.service.ts`)**: **Why?** To encapsulate the logic for resolving a domain to a namespace. This service would likely query the database or read a configuration map.
    - `backend/src/db/schema.ts` (Potentially New Table or Extension to `apis` Table)**: **Why?** You'll need to store the mapping between domains and namespaces. This could be a new table (e.g., `domain_namespaces` {domain, namespace, api_id/group_id}) or an extension to the `apis` table if an API itself is directly associated with a domain and implies a namespace.
    - `backend/src/config/index.ts` or API config files**: **Why?** Alternatively, if the domain-to-namespace mapping is static or part of API config, it would be defined here. Task 12.2 suggests it's in API config.

- [ ] 12.2 Add `domainBindings` to API config schema**
    - `backend/src/config/index.ts`**: **Why?** The `ApiConfigSchema` (or a new schema referenced by it) will need to be updated to include a `domainBindings` field. This field would likely be an array of objects, each mapping a domain to a specific namespace or indicating that the API should be accessible under that domain.
    - `backend/src/db/schema.ts`**: **Why?** The `apis.configJson` column (or new dedicated columns) would store this updated API configuration. If `domainBindings` are stored separately for more efficient querying, a new table might be linked to `apis`.
    - `frontend/src/lib/schemas/api-schema.ts`**: **Why?** The frontend's representation of the API configuration schema will also need to be updated if there's a visual editor or frontend validation for these bindings.
    - `frontend/src/pages/orchestrator/ApiEditorPage.tsx`**: **Why?** The JSON editor (and any future visual editor) would need to understand and allow editing of this new `domainBindings` section in the API configuration.

- [ ] 12.3 Restrict dynamic route handler by domain-based namespace**
    - `backend/src/routes/api.ts` (or the dynamic routing mechanism)**: **Why?** The logic that dynamically registers or handles requests for imported/configured APIs will need to be modified. It will:
        1.  Access the resolved namespace from the Hono context (set in Task 12.1).
        2.  Filter or select the appropriate API configuration based on whether its `domainBindings` match the current request's domain and resolved namespace.
    - `backend/src/services/namespace.service.ts` (from 12.1)**: **Why?** This service might also provide functions to check if a given API is accessible under the current namespace.
    - `backend/src/db/schema.ts` (and API config files)**: **Why?** The route handler will query/read API configurations (which now include `domainBindings`) to make routing decisions.

- [ ] 12.4 Add log tagging by `domain` + `namespace`**
    - `backend/src/logging/middleware.ts`**: **Why?** The existing `loggerMiddleware` can be enhanced. After the namespace resolution middleware (from Task 12.1) runs, the `loggerMiddleware` can access the `domain` (from `Host` header) and `resolvedNamespace` (from `c.get('namespace')`) and add them to the log metadata.
    - `backend/src/logging/core.ts`**: **Why?** The `Logger` class's internal `log` method or the `logToConsole` / `logToDatabase` methods might be updated to consistently include these tags if they are passed in the metadata.
    - `backend/src/logging/database.ts`**: **Why?** The `logToDB` function will save this additional domain/namespace metadata into the `metadataJson` field of the `logs` table.
    - `backend/src/db/schema.ts`**: **Why?** The `logs.metadataJson` field will store this. No schema change needed unless you want dedicated columns for domain/namespace in the logs table for easier querying (which could be an optimization).
    - `backend/src/logging/types.ts`**: **Why?** The `LogEntry` interface might be updated to optionally include `domain` and `namespace` as top-level properties for clarity, though storing them in `metadata` is also fine.

- [ ] 12.5 Add fallback if domain unregistered (403 or default landing)**
    - The new middleware from Task 12.1 (`backend/src/index.ts`)**: **Why?** If the `namespace.service.ts` cannot resolve a domain to a namespace (i.e., the domain is not registered or mapped), this middleware should handle the fallback. It can either:
        *   Return a 403 Forbidden response directly.
        *   Set a flag in the context, and a subsequent global error handler or routing layer returns a 403.
        *   Redirect to a default landing page (less common for APIs, more for web apps).
    - `backend/src/services/namespace.service.ts`**: **Why?** This service will return a null or specific indicator when a domain is not found, which the middleware will use to trigger the fallback.

- [ ] 12.6 Add dashboard page: Domains & Namespaces overview**
    - `frontend/src/App.tsx`**: **Why?** To add a new route (e.g., `/orchestrator/domains` or `/settings/domains`) for this new dashboard page.
    - New Frontend Page (e.g., `frontend/src/pages/orchestrator/DomainManagementPage.tsx`)**: **Why?** This page will display the list of registered domains, their associated namespaces, and potentially the APIs/services linked to them. It will allow for CRUD operations on these mappings.
    - `frontend/src/layouts/DashboardLayout.tsx`**: **Why?** To add a new navigation item in the sidebar pointing to this new page.
    - New Backend Endpoints (e.g., in `backend/src/routes/dcr.ts` or a new `backend/src/routes/domains.ts`)**: **Why?** CRUD endpoints are needed for the frontend to manage domain-to-namespace mappings (e.g., `GET /dcr/domains`, `POST /dcr/domains`, `DELETE /dcr/domains/:domainName`).
    - `backend/src/db/schema.ts` (and the table from 12.1)**: **Why?** The backend endpoints will interact with the database table that stores domain-namespace mappings.

- [ ] 12.7 Allow domain-only client registrations (no username/password)**
    - This is an Authentication/Authorization task.**
    - New Backend Auth Middleware (or modification to existing auth logic)**: **Why?** This middleware, running after domain/namespace resolution, would check if the request's resolved namespace/domain is associated with a "domain-only client." If so, it might bypass traditional user authentication or apply a specific set of permissions associated with that domain/client.
    - `backend/src/db/schema.ts` (Potentially New Table: `domain_clients` or `tenants`)**: **Why?** A new table to store these "domain-only clients" or "tenants", linking a domain (and/or namespace) to a set of permissions or an API key. This table might include fields like `domain`, `namespace`, `client_id`, `api_key_hash`, `associated_permissions_json`.
    - `backend/src/routes/dcr.ts` or `backend/src/routes/admin.ts`**: **Why?** Endpoints would be needed to manage these domain-client registrations (create, list, revoke API keys for domains).
    - `frontend/src/pages/orchestrator/DomainManagementPage.tsx` (from 12.6)**: **Why?** The UI to manage these domain-client registrations could be part of the domains & namespaces overview page.

- [ ] 12.8 Securely reject cross-namespace traffic from domain routes**
    - The domain/namespace resolution middleware (from Task 12.1)**: **Why?** This middleware sets the `resolvedNamespace` in the Hono context.
    - API Route Handlers (`backend/src/routes/api.ts` or dynamic router)**: **Why?** When an API endpoint is invoked, the handler must check if the target API/resource legitimately belongs to or is accessible by the `resolvedNamespace` from the context. If an API is configured with `domainBindings` (from 12.2) for `domainA` mapping to `namespaceA`, and a request comes via `domainA`, then only resources within `namespaceA` should be accessible.
    - `backend/src/services/namespace.service.ts`**: **Why?** Might provide utility functions like `isApiInNamespace(apiId, namespace)` or `getNamespaceForApi(apiId)`.
    - `backend/src/db/schema.ts`**: **Why?** The `apis` table (with `domainBindings` in `configJson` or a separate mapping table) will be the source of truth for which APIs belong to which namespaces/domains.
    - Authorization Logic (could be part of auth middleware or specific to route handlers)**: **Why?** After authentication, an authorization step would confirm that the authenticated identity (be it a user or a domain-client) has rights to access the requested resource *within the current namespace*.

This should cover the new tasks with the necessary context!
```

## 11. Deployment Prep

- [ ] 11.1 Dockerize backend + frontend**
    - New File: `backend/Dockerfile`**: **Why?** To define the build and runtime environment for the backend Node.js/Hono application.
    - New File: `frontend/Dockerfile`**: **Why?** To define the build process for the Vite/React frontend (e.g., `npm run build`) and serve the static assets (e.g., using Nginx or a simple static server).
    - New File: `docker-compose.yml` (at project root)**: **Why?** To orchestrate the backend, frontend, and potentially a database (like Postgres for production-like testing) for local development and testing of the containerized setup.

- [ ] 11.2 Setup production-ready `.env` and config**
    - `backend/.env.example`**: **Why?** This file serves as the template. You will create a production `.env` file based on this, filling in actual production values (database URLs, JWT secrets, AWS keys, etc.).
    - `backend/config/orchestrator.json`**: **Why?** Review and adjust default settings for production (e.g., `logLevel` to 'info' or 'warn', secure `jwtSecret` if not solely in .env, sensible `defaultRetries` and `defaultCacheTtl`). Consider a `orchestrator.prod.json` if overrides are needed.
    - `backend/src/env.ts`**: **Why?** Ensure all environment variables required for production (especially S3 keys, production database URL, JWT_SECRET) are defined in the `envSchema` and properly validated.
    - `backend/src/config.ts`**: **Why?** Review how configurations are loaded and merged, ensuring it behaves correctly in a production environment (e.g., file paths, overrides).

- [ ] 11.3 Add system healthcheck endpoint (`/status`)**
    - `backend/src/routes/api.ts`**: **Why?** The `/health` endpoint already exists.
    - `backend/src/routes/dcr.ts`**: **Why?** The `/status` endpoint exists and provides more details like DB status, uptime, and memory. This task involves reviewing these endpoints, ensuring they check all critical dependencies (database, cache, any external services the DCR relies on directly), and return appropriate status codes for monitoring tools.

- [ ] 11.4 Deploy on AWS EC2, attach S3 bucket**
    - `backend/.env` (Production version)**: **Why?** This file on the EC2 instance will contain the S3 bucket name and AWS credentials for the log archiving feature.
    - `backend/src/scripts/archive-logs.ts`**: **Why?** The S3 archiving logic within this script will be used.
    - `backend/src/logging/manager.ts`**: **Why?** The `archiveToS3` method needs to be fully implemented.
    - `Dockerfile`s (from Task 11.1)**: **Why?** These will be used to build the images deployed to EC2.
    - (New) Deployment Scripts (e.g., shell scripts, Ansible playbooks, Terraform configs)**: **Why?** These would be created to automate the deployment process to EC2, configure the environment, and set up S3 bucket policies if needed. Not directly in the codebase but essential for this task.

This should give a comprehensive mapping of tasks to the relevant parts of your codebase!
